   - Direct Path: A -> B -> T
   - Path via E: A -> E -> B -> T

   - A -> B -> T
   - A -> E -> B -> T
   - A -> E -> F -> B -> T
   - A -> S -> F -> B -> T

2. **Determine Critical Edges:**
   To ensure that none of these paths remain, we need to remove edges that will break all possible paths between A and T such that there is no alternative route remaining.

3. **Minimal Cut Set:**
   - Removing edge (A, B) breaks the paths directly connected through B.
   - Removing edge (E, B) ensures E cannot connect to T through B.
   - Removing edge (F, B) prevents the connection path through F to B.

   This set of edges must be sufficient to disconnect A and T. By removing (A, B), (F, B), and (E, B), all mentioned paths are broken and no other alternative remains.

Conclusion:
Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(A, B), (F, B), (E, B)].

   - Additionally, removing (B, F) ensures that there are no alternative paths through E or T.
